Yeah, my sleep has been pretty bad. I can get into bed completely exhausted from the day, but I just lie there... my mind just keeps running through bills and work schedules and everything else. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is that I've just stopped caring about everything I used to look forward to. Um, my fishing gear is still sitting untouched out in the garage. And the woodshop... it just feels like a chore to even walk in there lately. Even when my kids try to get me to play chess, I’m really just going through the motions. I just don't have any interest in any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't know if sad is the right word. Um, I haven't really noticed feeling sad or down, honestly. It's not like I'm walking around crying or anything like that. It's just... I guess I just feel pretty flat. Like I was saying with the hobbies, I just don't really feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in pleasure when you do engage in activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was saying a minute ago, even when I do force myself to do something—like sitting down for a game of chess with the kids—I don't get anything out of it anymore. I used to really enjoy it, but now there's just no satisfaction. I'm just staring at the board waiting for it to be over. Same with the woodshop... if I actually try to go out there and build something, it's just work. I just don't have the desire to eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty bad. I can get into bed completely exhausted from the day, but I just lie there... my mind just keeps running through bills and work schedules and everything else. I just lay there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to get up and drive to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean... I'm definitely tired from the lack of sleep, but physically? No, I don't think I'm moving any slower. Once I'm at the site, I just push through and do what needs to get done. It seems like if anything goes wrong—whether it's a missed appointment, a cost overrun, or a safety issue on site—I just take it as proof that I'm useless. I honestly feel like everyone would be better off without me in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. On those early drives to the site, before anyone else is even awake... I just catch myself thinking it would be a lot easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes, um... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me when it happens, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better or had a little more interest in things?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's... it's just been a constant low. I can't think of a single day in the last couple of weeks where I woke up feeling any better or actually wanted to do anything. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is that I've just stopped caring about everything I used to look forward to. Um, my fishing gear is still sitting untouched out in the garage. And the woodshop... it just feels like a chore to even walk in there lately. Even when my kids try to get me to play chess, I’m really just going through the motions. I just don't have any interest in any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't know if sad is the right word. Um, I haven't really noticed feeling sad or down, honestly. It's not like I'm walking around crying or anything like that. It's just... I guess I just feel pretty flat. Like I was saying with the hobbies, I just don't really feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in pleasure when you do engage in activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was saying a minute ago, even when I do force myself to do something—like sitting down for a game of chess with the kids—I don't get anything out of it anymore. I used to really enjoy it, but now there's just no satisfaction. I'm just staring at the board waiting for it to be over. Same with the woodshop... if I actually try to go out there and build something, it's just work. There's no enjoyment in any of it at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. I'd say about a year and seven months. It started right around the time my wife started having health problems, and everything at home just kind of changed after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt has had to come in a couple of notches. \n\nIt's mostly just because... well, I don't really have much of an appetite anymore. Most days, food just tastes like cardboard to me. So, um, I'll usually skip breakfast entirely, and then even if I've been out on a job site all day, I just end up picking at my dinner. I just don't have the desire to eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty bad. I can get into bed completely exhausted from the day, but I just lie there... my mind just keeps running through bills and work schedules and everything else. Or, if I do manage to fall asleep, I almost always wake up around three or four in the morning anyway. After that, I can't get back to sleep. I just lay there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to get up and drive to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean... I'm definitely tired from the lack of sleep, but physically? No, I don't think I'm moving any slower. Once I'm at the site, I just push through and do what needs to get done. I haven't really noticed feeling like I'm in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just keep thinking I've failed my wife and my kids. And the guys at work, too. It seems like if anything goes wrong—whether it's a missed appointment, a cost overrun, or a safety issue on site—I just take it as proof that I'm useless. I honestly feel like everyone would be better off without me in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. On those early drives to the site, before anyone else is even awake... I just catch myself thinking it would be a lot easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes, um... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me when it happens, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better or had a little more interest in things?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's... it's just been a constant low. I can't think of a single day in the last couple of weeks where I woke up feeling any better or actually wanted to do anything. Every day just feels exactly the same. Heavy and exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless. If anything, it's the opposite. I just feel so drained all the time, like it takes everything I have just to move or get out of a chair. I haven't had any extra energy to feel fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I'm moving slower, no. It's... it's mostly just what I was saying a minute ago about being so drained. The effort is definitely there—like it takes a lot just to pull myself out of the truck when I get to a site. But once I'm actually up and doing something, I think I move at my normal pace. I haven't really noticed my body lagging or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I used to really enjoy it, but now there's just no satisfaction. I'm just staring at the board waiting for it to be over. Same with the woodshop... if I actually try to go out there and build something, it's just work. There's no enjoyment in any of it at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. I'd say about a year and seven months. It started right around the time my wife started having health problems, and everything at home just kind of changed after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt has had to come in a couple of notches. \n\nIt's mostly just because... well, I don't really have much of an appetite anymore. Most days, food just tastes like cardboard to me. So, um, I'll usually skip breakfast entirely, and then even if I've been out on a job site all day, I just end up picking at my dinner. I just don't have the desire to eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty bad. I can get into bed completely exhausted from the day, but I just lie there... my mind just keeps running through bills and work schedules and everything else. Or, if I do manage to fall asleep, I almost always wake up around three or four in the morning anyway. After that, I can't get back to sleep. I just lay there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to get up and drive to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean... I'm definitely tired from the lack of sleep, but physically? No, I don't think I'm moving any slower. Once I'm at the site, I just push through and do what needs to get done. I haven't really noticed feeling like I'm in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just keep thinking I've failed my wife and my kids. And the guys at work, too. It seems like if anything goes wrong—whether it's a missed appointment, a cost overrun, or a safety issue on site—I just take it as proof that I'm useless. I honestly feel like everyone would be better off without me in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. On those early drives to the site, before anyone else is even awake... I just catch myself thinking it would be a lot easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes, um... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me when it happens, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better or had a little more interest in things?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's... it's just been a constant low. I can't think of a single day in the last couple of weeks where I woke up feeling any better or actually wanted to do anything. Every day just feels exactly the same. Heavy and exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless. If anything, it's the opposite. I just feel so drained all the time, like it takes everything I have just to move or get out of a chair. I haven't had any extra energy to feel fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I'm moving slower, no. It's... it's mostly just what I was saying a minute ago about being so drained. The effort is definitely there—like it takes a lot just to pull myself out of the truck when I get to a site. But once I'm actually up and doing something, I think I move at my normal pace. I haven't really noticed my body lagging or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about actually acting on those thoughts of death or dying, or made any plans to do so?\n\nPatient: Well... I don't have a set plan or anything like that. But on those early drives to the job sites, before anyone else is really awake, I catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes... the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It scares me, honestly, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're pacing back and forth or unable to sit still when you're at home or elsewhere?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said before, I really don't have the energy for that. I mostly just sit or lie down when I'm at home. I don't feel any urge to pace or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Or, if I do manage to fall asleep, I almost always wake up around three or four in the morning anyway. After that, I can't get back to sleep. I just lay there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to get up and drive to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean... I'm definitely tired from the lack of sleep, but physically? No, I don't think I'm moving any slower. Once I'm at the site, I just push through and do what needs to get done. I haven't really noticed feeling like I'm in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just keep thinking I've failed my wife and my kids. And the guys at work, too. It seems like if anything goes wrong—whether it's a missed appointment, a cost overrun, or a safety issue on site—I just take it as proof that I'm useless. I honestly feel like everyone would be better off without me in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. On those early drives to the site, before anyone else is even awake... I just catch myself thinking it would be a lot easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes, um... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me when it happens, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better or had a little more interest in things?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's... it's just been a constant low. I can't think of a single day in the last couple of weeks where I woke up feeling any better or actually wanted to do anything. Every day just feels exactly the same. Heavy and exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless. If anything, it's the opposite. I just feel so drained all the time, like it takes everything I have just to move or get out of a chair. I haven't had any extra energy to feel fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I'm moving slower, no. It's... it's mostly just what I was saying a minute ago about being so drained. The effort is definitely there—like it takes a lot just to pull myself out of the truck when I get to a site. But once I'm actually up and doing something, I think I move at my normal pace. I haven't really noticed my body lagging or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about actually acting on those thoughts of death or dying, or made any plans to do so?\n\nPatient: Well... I don't have a set plan or anything like that. But on those early drives to the job sites, before anyone else is really awake, I catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes... the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It scares me, honestly, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're pacing back and forth or unable to sit still when you're at home or elsewhere?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said before, I really don't have the energy for that. I mostly just sit or lie down when I'm at home. I don't feel any urge to pace or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or jittery to the point where you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's actually the opposite. I don't feel jittery or anxious like that. Honestly, I'm just too exhausted to feel restless or have the energy to fidget. When I'm not working, I'm just trying to stay still and rest.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a little bit ago... that's exactly what I meant about those early morning drives to the job sites. I just get this heavy feeling and start thinking it would be a lot easier if I just didn't make it there at all. So, yes, I definitely have thoughts like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

Um, I haven't really noticed feeling sad or down, honestly. It's not like I'm walking around crying or anything like that. It's just... I guess I just feel pretty flat. Like I was saying with the hobbies, I just don't really feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in pleasure when you do engage in activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was saying a minute ago, even when I do force myself to do something—like sitting down for a game of chess with the kids—I don't get anything out of it anymore. I used to really enjoy it, but now there's just no satisfaction. I'm just staring at the board waiting for it to be over. Same with the woodshop... if I actually try to go out there and build something, it's just work. There's no enjoyment in any of it at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. I'd say about a year and seven months. It started right around the time my wife started having health problems, and everything at home just kind of changed after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt has had to come in a couple of notches. \n\nIt's mostly just because... well, I don't really have much of an appetite anymore. Most days, food just tastes like cardboard to me. So, um, I'll usually skip breakfast entirely, and then even if I've been out on a job site all day, I just end up picking at my dinner. I just don't have the desire to eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty bad. I can get into bed completely exhausted from the day, but I just lie there... my mind just keeps running through bills and work schedules and everything else. Or, if I do manage to fall asleep, I almost always wake up around three or four in the morning anyway. After that, I can't get back to sleep. I just lay there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to get up and drive to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean... I'm definitely tired from the lack of sleep, but physically? No, I don't think I'm moving any slower. Once I'm at the site, I just push through and do what needs to get done. I haven't really noticed feeling like I'm in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just keep thinking I've failed my wife and my kids. And the guys at work, too. It seems like if anything goes wrong—whether it's a missed appointment, a cost overrun, or a safety issue on site—I just take it as proof that I'm useless. I honestly feel like everyone would be better off without me in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. On those early drives to the site, before anyone else is even awake... I just catch myself thinking it would be a lot easier if I didn't make it there. It actually scares me when it happens, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better or had a little more interest in things?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. And sometimes... the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is that I've just stopped caring about everything I used to look forward to. Um, my fishing gear is still sitting untouched out in the garage. And the woodshop... it just feels like a chore to even walk in there lately. Even when my kids try to get me to play chess, I’m really just going through the motions. I just don't have any interest in any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: I don't know if sad is the right word. Um, I haven't really noticed feeling sad or down, honestly. It's not like I'm walking around crying or anything like that. It's just... I guess I just feel pretty flat. Like I was saying with the hobbies, I just don't really feel much of anything at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a decrease in pleasure when you do engage in activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was saying a minute ago, even when I do force myself to do something—like sitting down for a game of chess with the kids—I don't get anything out of it anymore. I used to really enjoy it, but now there's just no satisfaction. I'm just staring at the board waiting for it to be over. Same with the woodshop... if I actually try to go out there and build something, it's just work. There's no enjoyment in any of it at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now. I'd say about a year and seven months. It started right around the time my wife started having health problems, and everything at home just kind of changed after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. I haven't been trying to lose weight or anything, but my belt has had to come in a couple of notches. \n\nIt's mostly just because... well, I don't really have much of an appetite anymore. Most days, food just tastes like cardboard to me. So, um, I'll usually skip breakfast entirely, and then even if I've been out on a job site all day, I just end up picking at my dinner. I just don't have the desire to eat.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns over the past year and seven months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty bad. I can get into bed completely exhausted from the day, but I just lie there... my mind just keeps running through bills and work schedules and everything else. Or, if I do manage to fall asleep, I almost always wake up around three or four in the morning anyway. After that, I can't get back to sleep. I just lay there staring at the ceiling until it’s time to get up and drive to the site.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving more slowly or feeling like it's harder to get started on things than it used to be?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say I have. I mean... I'm definitely tired from the lack of sleep, but physically? No, I don't think I'm moving any slower. Once I'm at the site, I just push through and do what needs to get done. I haven't really noticed feeling like I'm in slow motion or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything or like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I just keep thinking I've failed my wife and my kids. And the guys at work, too. It seems like if anything goes wrong—whether it's a missed appointment, a cost overrun, or a safety issue on site—I just take it as proof that I'm useless. I honestly feel like everyone would be better off without me in charge.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. On those early drives to the site, before anyone else is even awake... I just catch myself thinking it would be a lot easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes, um... sometimes the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It actually scares me when it happens, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt somewhat better or had a little more interest in things?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It's... it's just been a constant low. I can't think of a single day in the last couple of weeks where I woke up feeling any better or actually wanted to do anything. Every day just feels exactly the same. Heavy and exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, like you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel restless. If anything, it's the opposite. I just feel so drained all the time, like it takes everything I have just to move or get out of a chair. I haven't had any extra energy to feel fidgety.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than you used to, or like it's an effort to do physical things?\n\nPatient: I don't really think I'm moving slower, no. It's... it's mostly just what I was saying a minute ago about being so drained. The effort is definitely there—like it takes a lot just to pull myself out of the truck when I get to a site. But once I'm actually up and doing something, I think I move at my normal pace. I haven't really noticed my body lagging or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about actually acting on those thoughts of death or dying, or made any plans to do so?\n\nPatient: Well... I don't have a set plan or anything like that. But on those early drives to the job sites, before anyone else is really awake, I catch myself thinking it would just be easier if I didn't make it there. And sometimes... the thought of just steering off the road crosses my mind. It scares me, honestly, because it feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're pacing back and forth or unable to sit still when you're at home or elsewhere?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Like I said before, I really don't have the energy for that. I mostly just sit or lie down when I'm at home. I don't feel any urge to pace or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or jittery to the point where you can't sit still?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's actually the opposite. I don't feel jittery or anxious like that. Honestly, I'm just too exhausted to feel restless or have the energy to fidget. When I'm not working, I'm just trying to stay still and rest.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I mentioned a little bit ago... that's exactly what I meant about those early morning drives to the job sites. I just get this heavy feeling and start thinking it would be a lot easier if I just didn't make it there at all. So, yes, I definitely have thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of self-harm in addition to thoughts of death?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying about those early drives before anyone else is even awake... I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn't make it there. But sometimes it goes a bit further than just wishing it. Sometimes the thought of actually steering my truck off the road crosses my mind. And... that scares me, honestly, because it just feels a little too close to actually doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any psychomotor agitation or retardation?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what you mean by those terms, to be honest. If you're asking if I'm pacing around or... I don't know, moving in slow motion or something, then no. \n\nLike I said a minute ago, I'm just incredibly exhausted and feel heavy all the time, so I try to just stay still. But I haven't really noticed anything strange about how I actually move, if that's what you're getting at.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these thoughts of death or dying have been occurring daily, or are they more intermittent?\n\nPatient: It's pretty much daily. Like I was just saying about those early drives... it happens basically every morning before anyone else is awake. I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn't make it there. And that specific thought about steering off the road—it happens often enough that it really scares me. It just feels too close for comfort. So, yeah, I'd say it's an everyday thing at this point.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
